எனவே, மாநாட்டுச் சிறப்பு, கழகத்தவருக்கு மகிழ்ச்சி
தருவது மட்டுமல்ல, தங்கள் மமதைக்கு நாடு இனியும் இட
மளிக்கும் என்று எண்ணும் மந்த மதியினருக்கு, ஓர் அபாய
அறிவிப்பாகும். அன்று அங்கு கூடிய மாபெருங் கூட்டம்,
வழக்கமாகக் கட்சிகள் எத்தகைய 'ஆசாபாசங்களில்' சிக்கிக்
கொள்வது வாடிக்கையோ, அவ்விதமான 'ஆசாபாசங்களை'
அதாவது, அரசியல் சூதாட்டம், பதவி வேட்டை எனும் தன்மை
களை விட்டொழித்த கூட்டம் என்பதை உணர்ந்தால்தான்
வளர்ந்துள்ள சக்தி எத்தகையது, அதன் விளைவு யாதாக
இருக்கும் என்பது புரியும். முப்புரியினர், வேதத்தின் உட்
பொருளை எல்லாம் உணர்ந்தவர்கள் என்று பெருமை பேசு
கின்றனர் - ஆனால், அவர்களுக்கு, இந்த உண்மை மட்டும்
இன்னமும் புரிந்ததாகத் தெரியவில்லை. புரிந்திருக்குமானால்
‘பாரத தேவி’ 11-தேதி இதழில், பழைய பித்தத்தைக் கக்கி
யிருக்காது - மற்றோர் முப்புரி, புல்லேந்தும் கையால் வாளேந்து
வோம் என்று பிதற்றியிருக்காது, நாடு இன்னுள்ள நிலையை
அவர்கள் இன்னமும் புரிந்து கொள்ளாததாலேயே, இந்தப்
பித்தம் அவர்களுக்குக் குறையவில்லை. தூத்துக்குடி மாநா
டாவது அவர்களுக்குத் தெளிவை உண்டாக்கட்டும்.

ஆபாசங்களுக்குக் கட்டுபட்டு விட்டால், அரசியல்
சூதாட்டத்தில் ஈடுபட்டு விட்டால், வளர்ந்து வரும் சக்தி சிதறும்
சிதையும், என்ற சித்தாந்தம் சேலம் மாநாட்டிலே, நாம் பெற்ற
பெரும் பாடம். கழகம் இனியும் அதே போக்கிலேதான் செல்லும்
என்பதைத் தலைவர் தெளிவாக்கி விட்டார். எனவே, பச்சைப்
பசேலெனக் கழகம் இருப்பது கண்டு, பறந்து வந்து தங்கும்
அரசியல் பட்டுப்பூச்சிகளுக்கும், வெட்டுக்கிளிகளுக்கும் கழகம்
பலியிடப்படமாட்டாது என்பது தெளிவாகி விட்டது.

ஆபாசத்தை அறுத்துவிட்டு, ஆர்வத்தை அதிகப்படுத்திக்
கொண்டு, கூட்டத்திலே ஆள்சேருவது ஏது என்று நிலையி
லிருந்து விடுபட்டு, கூட்டம் ஒரு இலட்சம் இருக்கும், இரண்டு
இலட்சம் இருக்கும் என்று மகிழ்ச்சியுடன் மதிப்பிடும் அளவுக்கு
நிலைமை முன்னேறி, இதெல்லாம் தேர்தலுக்காக, பதவிக்காக
பட்டத்துக்காக, காண்டிராக்டுக்காக என்ற பழிச் சொல்லி
லிருந்து விடுபட்டு இது சமுதாயத்தைப் புது உருவாக்க, அறிவுத்
துறையிலே புரட்சியை உண்டாக்க என்று கூறத்தக்க முறைக்கு
முன்னேறியுள்ள இந்த மாபெரும் சக்தியை உதாசீனப்படுத்து
பவர், உலுத்தராக இருக்க முடியுமே தவிர, உள்ளத்தின் போக்கை
யூகித்துரைக்கக் கூடியவர்களாக இருக்க முடியாது. “ஆகட்டும்;

### Image 2: 002-278c27cd33.png

அடுத்த தேர்தலிலே, அரைகோடி ஒரு கோடி செலவிட்டாவது
இதுகளைத் தொலைத்துவிடுகிறேன்” என்று கூறவும் முடியாது.
பதவி பிடிக்கும் சுயநலமிகள் இவர்கள், பாரீர் இன்னின்னார்
இன்னின்ன பதவிக்குப் பல்லிளிக்கிறார்கள்” என்று வெட்ட
வெளிச்சமாக்கிக் காட்டி, 'இதுகளை வெளியே தலைகாட்ட
முடியாதபடி செய்து விடுகிறோம்' என்று வீம்பு பேசவும்
இடமில்லை.

“உரத்த குரலிலே கூவிடுவோருக்கு உயர்ந்த பதவி கொடுத்து
விட்டு, செல்லப்பிள்ளையாக்கிக் கொள்வோம்" என்ற தந்திரம்
புரியவும் இடமில்லை. ஏனெனில், திராவிடர் கழகம், இத்தகைய
அசாபாசங்களுக்கு அப்பாற்பட்டது - சேலத்தில் புடம் போட்ட
தங்கம், மாற்றுக் குறையாததாகி விட்டது. இந்த மகத்தான
உண்மையை, “முகத்திலுதித்த” முத்தண்ணாக் கூட்டம், சற்று
உணர வேண்டும்.

“இருக்கட்டும் இருக்கட்டும், இதுகளின்மீது காங்கிரசை
மோதவிட்டு, இரு மண்டை களிலிருந்தும் குருதி கொட்டக்கண்டு
மகிழ்வோம்” என்ற நயவஞ்சக நினைப்புக்கும், இடமளிக்கவில்லை
கழகம். ஏனெனில், காங்கிரஸ் கட்சியுடன், தேர்தலிலோ, பதவி
களிலோ போட்டியிடும் எண்ணத்தையும் அறவே நீக்கிவிட்ட
நிலை பெற்றுவிட்டது.

எனவேதான், ‘ஆசாபாசம், அறுபட்ட நிலையை நாம் மிக
மிக ஜீவசக்தி தருவது என்று கூறுகிறோம்.

கழகத்தின் நோக்கமிருக்கட்டும், நிலை இருக்கட்டும், கழகத்
துக்குள்ளே பிளவும் பேதமும், அதிகமாகி வருகிறதாமே - என்று
பேசுவதன் மூலம் திருப்தியைத் தருவித்துக் கொள்ளலாம் என்ற
எண்ணத்தையும் விட்டுவிட வேண்டும்; முன்பு புல்லேந்தி இனி
வில்லேந்தலாம் என்று எண்ணும் கூட்டத்தினர். பேதம் உண்டு.
ஆனால், அந்தப் பேதம், பேதப்பட்டுள்ள இரு பிரிவினரும்,
இடையே உள்ளவர்களின் எண்ணிக்கையைக் கணக்கெடுக்கவும்,
தன்மையை உணர்ந்து கொள்ளவும், வசதி தருமே; ஒழிய,
எதிரிக்கு இடந்தருவதற்கு எள்ளவும் உபயோகப்படாது. சிற்சில
சந்தர்ப்பங்களைச் சிலர், எப்படி எப்படி உபயோகித்துக்
கொள்வார்கள் என்பதைக் கண்டறியும் ஒரு போட்டிப் பந்தயம்
போன்றதே, பேதம். இது எந்த வகையிலும், மாற்றார்க்கு இடந்
தரும் மன நிலையை ஏற்படுத்தாது.

எனவே, தூத்துக்குடி மாநாடு தரும் பாடம், என்ன
என்பதை நாட்டிலே நாலு ஜாதி வகுத்தவர்களும், நாடாள்

### Image 3: 003-cc6596e983.png

- Image: `katturaigal/latchiya_varalaru_6/003-cc6596e983.png`

வோரும் உணர வேண்டும். அங்கு கூடிய மக்களின் உள்ளத்திலே
இருந்ததெல்லாம்,

ஜாதி பேதமற்ற - பொருளாதார பேதமற்ற - அறிவுத்
தெளிவுபெற்ற அன்னியனின் சுரண்டலுக்கு
ஆளாகாத ஓர் இனம். அதன் உரிமையுள்ள
ஆட்சியை அமைத்துக் கொண்டு, உலக
மன்றத்திலே வீற்றிருக்க வேண்டும்.

என்பதுதான். இந்த மூலக் கருத்தை இனி முறியடிக்க முடியாது
- இதன் வளர்ச்சியைத் தடுக்கவும் முடியாது. இக் கருத்து, கழகக்
கோட்டத்தோடும் நின்றுவிடவில்லை - கதர்ச் சட்டையைத்
துளைத்துக் கொண்டு, காங்கிரஸ் நண்பர்களின் உள்ளங்களிலே
இடம்பெற்று வருகிறது. நாட்டிலே நல்லறிவாளர் பேசும்
பேச்சிலே, இந்தக் கருத்து வெளிவருகிறது. புதிய கவிதைகள்,
புதிய நாடகங்கள், புதிய சினிமாக்கள், எதிலும் இந்த மூலக்
கருத்து தெளிவாகத் தெரிய ஆரம்பித்து விட்டது. எனவே,
இந்தக் கருத்தைக் குலைத்துவிட முடியும் என்று கனவு காணும்
நிலையை விட்டு விட்டு, காலத்தின் தாக்குதல் பலமாக விழா
முன்பே, கருத்திலே தெளிவைக்கொண்டு நீதியை நிலைநாட்ட
முற்பட வேண்டுகிறோம். கரங்களிலேயும் கருத்திலேயும் இரும்புச்
சங்கிலி போட்டு விட்டிருக்கிறோமே, எங்கனம் அந்த அடிமை
விடுபட முடியும் என்று எண்ணியவர்கள் கண்முன்பு, இன்று,
அறுபட்ட தளைகளைக் கழற்றி வீசிடாமலும்கூட, திராவிடன்
ஏறு நடை நடந்து வந்து, எதிரே நின்று,

இது என் நாடு! - இயற்கை, இதைப் பொன்னாடு
ஆக்கும்! - இங்க ஜாதி கூடாது! - மதத்தின்
கொடுமை கூடாது பொருளாதார பேதம் கூடாது!
- வடநாட்டுச் சுரண்டல் கூடாது! உரிமை
வேண்டும் புது வாழ்வு வேண்டும்! என்று
கூறுகிறான்.

காற்றையும், கடலையும் கேட்டுக் கலங்கிய திராவிடனின்
செவியிலே, ஒரு இலட்சம் மக்களின் உணர்ச்சியும், சூளுரையும்
விழுந்தது! அவன் வெற்றிப் பாதையில் நடக்கிறான் - அந்தப்
பயணத்தைத் தடுக்க முடியாது - வெற்றியைக் கெடுக்க இயலாது
- அவன் விழிப்புற்றான், எழுச்சியுற்றான், கூட்டினை விட்டுக்
கிளம்பிய சிங்கமெனக் காணப்படுகிறான். முப்புரியினருக்கு
'ஞானக்கண்' உண்டென்று வீம்பு பேசுபவர், அந்த ஞானக்கண்

### Image 4: 004-79c4fc2b98.png

- Image: `katturaigal/latchiya_varalaru_6/004-79c4fc2b98.png`

கொண்டுகூட அல்ல, சாதாரண ஊனக் கண்ணும், சராசரி மூளை
பலமும் கொண்டு கண்டாலே போதும்; மறைக்க முடியாத
உண்மையைக் காணலாம்!

மரண சாசனம்

திராவிடர் கழகத்திலுள்ள தோழர்கள் ஒவ்வொருவரும்,
தயாரித்துக்கொள்ள வேண்டிய மரண சாசனம் இது. வீழ்ச்சி
யுற்ற இனத்தை எழுச்சி பெறச் செய்துவிட்டோம்; எந்த விலை
கொடுத்தேனும், விடுதலையைப் பெற்றுத் தீரவேண்டிய
கட்டத்தில் வந்துவிட்டோம்.

வெட்டும் குத்தும், இனி நம்மை விரைந்து தேடி வரும்;
வாழ்வுக்கும், சாவுக்கும், இடையே அமைந்துள்ள ஊஞ்சலி
லேயே நாம் உலாவ வேண்டியவர்களாக இருப்போம். வைகைக்
கரையிலே சென்ற கிழமை நடந்த அமளி, நமக்கு அறிவுறுத்தும்
பாடம் அதுதான்; நாம் இருக்குமட்டும் நமது ஆதிக்கத்துக்கு
ஆபத்துதான் என்பதை ஐயந்திரிபற அறிந்துகொண்ட
வர்ணாஸ்ரமம், நாம் செத்தால் மட்டுமே, தான் இன்னும்
கொஞ்ச காலத்துக்கேனும் ஜீவித்திருக்க முடியும் என்று நன்கு
தெரிந்து கொண்டு, நம்மைக் கொல்லக் கோர நாட்டியம்
செய்தது. நமது இரத்தத்தையும் கொஞ்சம் குடித்து ருசி
பார்த்தது. நமது வளர்ச்சியின் அறிகுறி நமக்குமட்டுமல்ல,
பிராமண சேவா சங்கத்தாரும் தெரிந்திருக்கிறது. அவர்களின்
மகஜர், நமது வளர்ச்சிக்கு ஆரியம் தரும் நற்சாட்சிப் பத்திரம்.

எல்லாம் சரி, ஆனால், இத்தகைய அறப்போரில் சேதம்
நேரிடுகிறதே. இரத்தம் வீணாக்கப்படுகிறதே என்று எண்ணு
கிறார்கள் சிலர். அவர்களுக்கு ஒரு வார்த்தை! நாம் எடுத்துக்
கொண்டுள்ள மகத்தான காரியத்தின் தன்மையை ஒரு கணம்
சிந்திக்கவேண்டும்.

யுகயுகமாக இருந்துவருவதாகக் கூறப்படும் ஏற்பாடுகளை
நாம், திருத்தி அமைக்க விரும்புகிறோம். மமதை மலைக்கு வேட்டு
வைக்கிறோம். நம்மீது சிறுசிறு துண்டுகள் சிதறி விழுந்து,
மண்டையைப் பிளக்கின்றன என்றால், நாம் வைத்த வேட்டு
மலையைப் பிளந்து வருகிறது என்று பொருள். மலையைப்

### Image 5: 005-9b05d38fd0.png

- Image: `katturaigal/latchiya_varalaru_6/005-9b05d38fd0.png`

பிளக்கும் காரியத்தில் இறங்கிவிட்டு, மலர் தலைமீது விழும்
என்று எதிர்பார்க்க முடியாததல்லவா? நம்மை நாமாகவே இந்தக்
காரியத்துக்கு ஒப்படைத்துவிட்டோம். உலகில் பல பாகங்
களிலே, இதற்கு ஒப்பான காரியம் செய்யப் புகுந்தவர்கள் பட்ட
பாடுகள், இன்று பல்கலைக் கழகங்களின் பாடப் புத்தகங்களாகி
விட்டன. அன்று சாக்ரடீஸ் குடித்த விஷம், இன்றுவரை, சாகா
நிலையைச் சாக்ரடீசுக்குத் தந்துவிட்டது. பழியையும் இழிவையும்,
எதிர்ப்பையும், ஆபத்தையும் தலைமீது ஏற்றுக் கொண்டு, பணி
புரிந்து சென்று, அந்தப் பணியின் பலனைப் பின் சந்ததியார்
அநுபவிக்கச் செய்யும் பரம்பரையில், நாம், சேர்ந்திருக்கிறோம்.
நமக்கு, இன்னலும் இடையூறும் இருக்கத்தான் செய்யும்; ஆனால்
நமது உழைப்பு ஒருநாளும் வீண் போகாது!

கடு விஷம் கொடுத்துக் கொல்லப்பட்டோர், காரிருட்
சிறையில் ஆயுட்கால முழுதும் தள்ளப்பட்டோர், கல்லால்
அடித்துத் துரத்தப்பட்டோர், சிலுவையில் அறையப்பட்டோர்,
சிறுத்தைக்கு இரையாக்கப்பட்டோர், கழுத்து நெரிக்கப்பட்
டோர், கனலில் தள்ளப்பட்டோர், கண்டதுண்டமாக்கப்
பட்டோர், நாட்டு மக்களாலேயே துரத்தி அடிக்கப்பட்டோர்,
நாதியற்றுப் போனோர் என்று இவ்விதமாகத்தான் இருக்கும்,
சமூகப் புரட்சிப் பணியிலே ஈடுபட்டவர்களுடைய வாழ்க்கை
வரலாறுகள். நாம் அந்த இனம். அவர்களெல்லாம் இன்று,
அறிஞர் உலகின் அணிமணிகளாயினர். நம்மையும், பின்சந்ததி
மறவாது.

காதிலே ஈயத்தைக் காய்ச்சி ஊற்றிய காலம், கட்டிப்
போட்டு வீட்டுக்குத் தீயிட்டகாலம், கிணற்றில் தள்ளிக் கல்லிட்ட
காலம், கண்களைத் தோண்டி எடுத்த காலம், நாவைத் துண்டித்த
காலம், கழுவிலேற்றிய காலம், தலையைக் கொய்த காலம்,
தணலில் தள்ளிய காலம் - இவையெல்லாம் இருந்தன. சீர்
திருத்தம் பேசியோர் இவைகளிலேதான் உழன்றனர். பெரும்
பாலானவர்கள் சாகவில்லை; கொல்லப்பட்டனர். கொல்லப்
பட்டதாலேயே அவர்கள் இன்று சாகாதவராக உள்ளனர்.
எனவேதான், மரணசாசனம் தயாரித்துக்கொண்டு இந்த
மகத்தான போராட்டத்திலே இறங்கவேண்டும் என்று
கூறுகிறோம்.

சமூகத்தில் அடிப்படை மாறுதலை விரும்பும் நாம்
கொல்லப் படக்கூடும் என்ற எண்ணத்திற்காக நாம் இப்பணி
நமக்கேன் என்றிருந்துவிடினும், 'சாவு' ஓய்வு எடுத்துக்

என்று தான் பதிகம் செவியில் ஒலிக்கும். செத்தால், செத்தது

தாக இருந்தது. இதன் பொருள் என்ன? மதுரைச் சம்பவத்தால்,

தோழர்களே! உங்கள் இரத்தம் வீணுக்குச் சிந்தப்படவில்லை.

அந்தச் சேதி எண்ணற்ற தமிழரின் இரத்தத்தில் கொதிப்பேற்றி

ஆகையினால் இருக்கும் இன்றே இன்னும் கொஞ்சம் இன

அவசரமான நிலைமை - இவ்வளவே! நாம், மரண சாசனம்
தயாரித்துவிட்டு, இந்த மகத்தான போராட்டத்தில், ஈடுபட்டு
விட்டோம்! எனவே, நமது இலட்சியம் - குறிக்கோள் எப்படியும்
வெற்றிபெற்றே தீருமென்பது உறுதி!

## English Translation

## Part 1

### Image 1: 001-bb7ac101f0.png

History of an Ideal

29 June 1947

### Image 2: 002-bb5364e774.png

History of an Ideal

“I have been saying for ten years that the Dravidar Kazhagam is not a party as it was when it bore the name Justice Party, but a movement for spreading its ideas. That is why I have been shaping the Kazhagam into an organization for propagating those ideas, so that people will regard it as a movement rather than as a party. For it is the Self-Respect Movement, which we began in 1925 and have been conducting since, that has taken over the Justice Party and is carrying on the movement's campaign; it is not in the hands of a separate body of party politicians. There may perhaps be some small alteration in the Self-Respect Movement's programmes, but in the Dravidar Kazhagam there is no change whatsoever in that movement's foundations or its principal policies. The Annadurai resolution at the Salem Conference already made this clear. Thus, the point is that the people for whom the Justice Party was founded and conducted—the Dravidian people—have accepted the Self-Respect Movement in its entirety and have largely become adherents of Self-Respect, while those who were not adherents have gradually slipped away. We may therefore say that, under the name Dravidar Kazhagam, the Self-Respect Movement is chiefly carrying on its own work of spreading the movement's ideas. That is why I repeatedly say that the Dravidar Kazhagam is a movement for propagating ideas, and not a party organization like Congress, the Communists, the Socialists, the Hindu Mahasabha, the Varnashrama Swarajya Sangam, the Brahmin Protection Association, the Liberal Association, the Muslim League, the Christian Association, the Depressed Classes, the Scheduled League, and others. To explain: this Kazhagam is an organization that, in people's intellectual life,

### Image 3: 003-1380f72045.png

social life, religion and beliefs about God, works to remove the follies, obstacles to progress and deficiencies that have persisted for so long; to bring all people together as one society; and, especially, to eradicate the social degradation, religious superstition and divisions of caste that afflict us, the Dravidian people, and to free us from Aryan oppression. These are the things it urges upon the people.

The Dravidar Kazhagam has neither an imaginary doctrine that we must obtain self-rule nor the mistaken notion that we must stand for election, secure a majority and capture ‘political power.’ For what prevails in the country today is itself called self-rule. The domination of the foreign white man—the British—has gone. In the Dravidian country, Dravidian people themselves are the first minister and the second minister; indeed, the cabinet has a Dravidian majority, with ten of its thirteen ministers said to be Dravidians. In these circumstances, what further meaning can there be in obtaining self-rule? Who is to obtain it? Capture political power? Who is to capture it from whom? How can political power hereafter pass into the hands of people who are not Dravidians? Thus, to speak of obtaining self-rule or capturing political power is henceforth meaningless talk, and to pursue these things is meaningless action.

In this province, we ourselves are now going to constitute more than ninety out of every hundred voters. Most ministers and officials are going to be Dravidians. There is no reason to doubt it. But the objection that may be raised is this: will that bring us a separate Dravidian country? Will the Dravidar Kazhagam's policies and programmes be put into practice? Let us suppose that this will not happen easily. But if we—the members of the Dravidar Kazhagam—enter the legislature, capture political power or become ministers, will a Dravidian country thereby come into being? Will the Kazhagam's policies and programmes

### Image 4: 004-727f978695.png

be carried out through political power? Considering the present circumstances, our view is that these are not things that can easily be achieved.

**Victory Is in Sight**

“Our work is to remove our degradation and the obstacles to our progress, to free ourselves from Aryanism and superstition, and to become rationalists and a society with self-respect. In doing such work, dealing with ordinary people is like dealing with fire. We must win their trust. We must reform them. This requires effective propagation of our ideas. It requires cooperation. We must unite. We have to bring together the people who lie scattered and shattered, gather everyone's strength into one, and put that strength to the test. ‘We shall no longer live as Shudras’ is the declaration of our ideal, our very breath.

Victory is visible before our eyes; it is no mirage. I am certain that, if we give it thought and care, we shall assuredly attain it.

Leader: Periyar

**Written in Tears**

**Dravidian Kings**

They did not, like Dharmaraja, lose their country at gambling.

They did not, like Harishchandra, give their country away to a sage.

**Dravidian Poets**

They did not write cosmic falsehoods about Atala, Sutala and Patala.

They did not compose tales claiming that God assumed every imaginable form.

### Image 5: 005-72b3b98a70.png

They did not say that rolling at someone's feet in worship would win divine favour.

They said: one community, one God.

**The Dravidians**

They did not surrender their spirit merely to stay alive.

They did not live as mean-spirited people without resolve.

They did not take deceit and intrigue as their weapons.

They gave even their lives for a life of freedom.

**Dravidam**

It had not been enslaved under foreign rule.

It had known neither famine, disease nor anguish.

It had not stood by and tolerated the suffering of working people.

It rose through labour and won its rightful place in the world.

**Dravidam: Today**

The Dravidian kings have disappeared; the people have forgotten their character.

Valour has withered! Worthless men have gained dominion.

Dravidian art and languages command no respect.

Dravidian industry lies dormant. The Dravidian sobs.

Dravidian wealth has become locked up in temples, flagstaffs, drumming and fanfare, useless activities, and foreign lands.

The Dravidian suffers as a labourer on tea, sugarcane and rubber plantations.

In every nook and corner of the Dravidian country there are Marwari shops, Gujarati warehouses, Multani establishments and Seths' companies.

All the great businesses are in the hands of northerners.

In the factories, there are no longer British masters—there are Banias!

### Image 6: 006-51593aaf54.png

Dalmia has a whole town near Tiruchirappalli.

In the Tiruppur–Coimbatore region, all the mill kings are reduced to singing the praises of the Asher Seths.

In Madras there is Sowcarpet; there is the Bharat Bank.

The bell that rings every day belongs to a northerner, Goenka.

Today the great buildings in Madras belong to northerners!

In a Dravidam reduced to this condition, everything from clothing to nails comes from the North. To say that it comes from there is to say that enormous sums of money go from here to the North.

Dravidam has lost its prosperity; it has poured even the little wealth that remains into the North and is wasting away with the disease of poverty. For it to raise its head, for its people to enjoy a full life, for those who went abroad as labourers to return, and for the standard of living to rise, there is no programme other than ours: a Dravidian country for the Dravidians!

We ask everyone outside the Dravidar Kazhagam to view this question, which has economics at its foundation, with eyes full of love for the country, rather than through a merely partisan lens.

Look at the Marwari who has wound coloured cloth around his head in coil upon coil and sits beside heaps of goods, examining his interest accounts and stroking his belly; and look at the Tamil who pulls a heavily laden cart in place of an ox! Look at the Seths riding in procession in motorcars whose polish has not faded; and look at the Tamil, in torn clothes, with a parched head and eyes that have lost their light, waiting for a ship ticket to go abroad as a labourer. Look at the lush green irrigated fields here and

### Image 7: 007-b1e0b94677.png

dry fields, and at the same time look at the Tamils emaciated by hunger, without even the strength to walk. Forget us and look at these sights. Forget even that we are proposing a programme; think for yourselves and propose one. Is it just that a country with an ancient greatness, a country rich in resources of many kinds, should today be joined to the North and suffer like this? Is it a crime to free it from that union, to stand independently with a government of its own, to increase the country's wealth and ensure that this wealth serves its own people? What programme can there be for this other than a Dravidian country for the Dravidians alone? If there is another, tell us!

Standing on a ship's deck, unable to find the means of supporting himself in his prosperous motherland, the Tamil says in anguish: “Here I am, going to Malaya, a country I have never seen. I am going to work in the forests. I am leaving my mother. Poverty is driving me from my motherland. I am going abroad as a labourer.” The ideal written with the tears he sheds is this: a Dravidian country for the Dravidians alone.

**A New History**

**11 September 1938**

We sang our battle song: “Tamil Nadu for the Tamils.”

**27 December 1938**

At the Tamils' Conference in Vellore, the future programme for Tamil Nadu was discussed.

**10 December 1939**

A celebration was held to explain the programme “Tamil Nadu for the Tamils” to the people of the country.

**2 July 1940**

A conference for the separation of Dravida Nadu was held in Kanchipuram, and a committee was appointed to pursue separation.

### Image 8: 008-1f4c9db5fa.png

**29 December 1938**

At the provincial Justice Conference in Madras, the demand that Tamil Nadu become a separate country was emphasized.

**20 August 1944**

At the provincial conference in Salem, the demand that Dravida Nadu become a separate country was made the party's fundamental programme, and the party's very name was changed to Dravidar Kazhagam.

**24–25 August 1940**

At the provincial conference in Tiruvarur, the programme that Dravida Nadu become a separate country was adopted as a resolution.

**29 September 1945**

At the provincial conference in Tiruchirappalli, the demand that Dravida Nadu become a separate country was emphasized once again.

**1 July 1947**

Celebration of Dravida Nadu Separation Day.

**We Said**

The Muslims have become so resolute that their thinking amounts to this: obtain Pakistan, or enter the graveyard. They are not guns without bullets.

*Viduthalai*, 16 December 1940

**We Said**

“The Pakistan scheme is dangerous; it is mischievous. Congress does not even regard it as a matter worthy of its consideration.”

Jawahar, October 1940

**What Happened?**

Pakistan has been obtained.

### Image 9: 009-58589d0e12.png

**Dravidar Kazhagam**

At the conference held in Salem on the 27th, the new name Dravidar Kazhagam for the South Indian Welfare Rights Association, and the programme of relinquishing titles and offices to form ranks of workers who would restore the country and end its afflictions, won the support of many thousands of ardent, energetic Dravidian champions. We do not regard that successful conference as merely a ceremony or a victory for one group; we regard it as an important, noteworthy stage in a movement's development. It adopted radical programmes—not for the satisfaction of having done so! We are not concerned about those who draw up programmes and then stand aloof. Such people have no concern for the programmes either. But the gathering of brave people who assembled there that day, ready to bear hardship and loss, wanted nothing less than a struggle for liberation! Those thousands upon thousands of fearless comrades have no interest in expounding the *Viveka Chintamani* or drawing up a schedule of political manoeuvres. They are not people who conduct their public lives by waiting for a favourable opportunity to acquire a title or office. “Must a great people with an illustrious past be ruined? Must a country that has its place in the pages of world history become a haunt of worthless men? Can we bear the name of men of courage without fighting to change this condition?” With this fire blazing within them, those champions wanted their rights! Yes, they wanted the right proclaimed in “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone.” The light in their eyes, the sound they raised that day, the sight of them slapping their shoulders, standing with chests held high and forming their ranks—these were signs of a people's awakening, the trumpet call of an army of liberation, the cry of those who understood the root of the matter,

### Image 10: 010-c44b2197b8.png

not a fashionable gathering that offered congratulations to a gracious government, bowed before the wealthy, and carried messages to lords and ladies. The country must understand this; we too must impress it upon our minds. *

Here was a people disregarded by the ruling group, mocked by rival parties, insulted by Aryans, tormented by northerners; a people who had lost their land and honour, been robbed of their wealth, called delusion to their aid, and, finding no way forward, lain on their knees before the arrogant. That day, it was a gathering of champions who took a great vow: “We must obtain liberation. I shall work for it! I shall give my life! I shall not be beguiled by anything that comes along under the name of pleasure. I shall fight! Either the garland of victory or the summons of death!”

It was not a gathering of pleasure-seekers who meet as a matter of routine, speak charmingly, display verbal bravery without bending or stooping, indulge the fancy that they have won, and then go off to a land of pleasure surrounded by women! It was a gathering of people who knew the nature of poverty! People who knew what hunger and starvation were!! Working people—but not fools who bear the feet of Brahminism. Not clowns who imagine that they can become crowned rulers without first defeating that Brahminism in religion and society. Noble people, worn and transformed by their labour for the community, gathered that day! They revealed their determination in a way that even the country's rulers would feel and understand. That day they wiped away the slur that this was a party lying about, yawning only for titles and offices. The grime upon the jewel was wiped away; its light had begun to emerge. Why a title? Why an office? Must the European and the Brahmin decide whether to confer a title upon the Tamil who once ruled the world? It was the Tamil who crossed the seas! The Tamil who engraved the tiger on the Himalayas! The Tamil who took Kadaram! The Tamil who sold golden cloth to Rome! The Tamil who savoured literature! The Tamil is a warrior whom every country admires; the Tamil strides like a proud bull! Even when faced with hardship, the Tamil smiles! Is a title for him to come from the masters who came to sell soap,

### Image 11: 011-c0e7e50301.png

combs and mirrors, and later began to rule? Why? What use is the title Rao Bahadur to someone who has not moved a little finger to remove the degrading title ‘Shudra’ that the Aryan imposed on us long ago? There are many who would take up these titles and offices only if they happened to fall at their feet! There are a few who talk of nothing else by day, dream of them at night, and think of them at every moment!! Their number is very small! As the English poet Shelley, whom we may call Britain's Bharathidasan, said: “They are few; we are very many!!” The many assembled and told them: “Either change your ways, or, if you cannot, make room for us to lead a separate life.” That is the meaning of the resolution to give up titles and offices! Commander Pandian supported this resolution and said that, above all, systematic organizational work within the party was necessary to make it practicable. “Yes! We shall do it!” the supporters replied.

As far as Tamil Nadu is concerned, the commanders have agreed to this constructive work. The responsibility for providing the necessary encouragement rests with comrades who feel the cause deeply. The young possess the ‘power’ to put it into practice. Go everywhere, from the smallest village to the largest city; tell people the news; demonstrate how many comrades you can enrol in the Dravidar Kazhagam. The leaders must be astonished at the ranks you assemble. We need ranks that will make Aryanism cry out and the world take notice! We need them quickly!! There is much work to do. The drum of liberation has sounded. The struggle for a government of our people—the final struggle—must be fought. Set out today to establish and strengthen the Dravidian camp!!

The day when Andhra and Kerala witness this momentum is not far off. The means to bring it about will certainly be devised.

The point is that our people's present condition must change, and Dravida Nadu must belong to the Dravidians alone. It is to

## Part 2

### Image 1: 001-3ad92cd9bc.png

accomplish this that we stand together under the leadership of a Periyar with a body full of vigour, a heart of adamant, a mind brimming with thought and practical experience in getting things done—a tireless man who sees no difference between prison and home. He has seen many fields of battle. He has conducted many struggles; he has rejected a life of luxury and lives the life of the poor. He will find many Thalamuthus and Natarajans. He has the charm to summon young people in such numbers that the ruling group will say in dismay, “They are coming like winged termites emerging from an anthill!” He is enough for us. Some others apparently need someone else! We need not concern ourselves with that. Periyar, who knows how to fight, calls the fighters: “Warriors! Come, come!” We want no other declaration—we have no need of one.

Come and work; do not ask me how you are to earn a living! Come and rescue your people; do not ask me whether we have the strength for it! Come to the struggle; do not ask me how it will end or when it will end. This is Periyar's declaration.

Those who want rest may stand aside. Those in reclining chairs may remain reclining. Lovers of office may step out of the way. Those prepared to lose even their lives to gain honour may come!! That is the message Salem sent forth.

**Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians Alone**

This is not the slogan of a party; it is the song of a people's heart! A fundamental truth. A truth accepted everywhere, by peoples of every kind. Those who think they must oppose this idea merely because we put it forward, and those who dismiss it, are doing harm not to us

### Image 2: 002-e3f3af3635.png

but to the country; they are betraying their own people.

Even tiny countries, countries without natural resources, countries that must live on borrowed resources, have obtained independent sovereignty. Here, however, a country that already possessed a rich literature two thousand years ago is today bound to another country and held captive. We call upon those brave people who consider it a great task to break, within their own lifetimes, the shackles fastened upon their motherland. We say: make “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone” known to people in every nook and corner—join the ranks.

To explain to everyone the blameless character of our policy, the necessity of our programme and the greatness of our ideal, the Dravidar Kazhagam has arranged to celebrate this coming 1 July as Dravidian Separation Day. We have not drawn up the programme “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians” without meaning: there are sound, irrefutable reasons for it.

Whoever they may be—those who condemn, ridicule, dismiss or disregard it—we ask them to sit down calmly, consider the reasons we offer for “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone,” and then reach a conclusion. We have stated these reasons many times. Now, as we celebrate a day devoted to this purpose, we state those same reasons to the people of the country once again.

1. India is a continent. It must therefore be divided into several countries. The European continent contains thirty-two separate countries. No one says that all Europe must be under one umbrella. Nor is there any necessity for India to remain under a single umbrella.

2. Before British rule was established, India was a continent containing fifty-six countries, each with its own government. It was only to make the conduct of their rule

### Image 3: 003-1dfaa2436e.png

more convenient that the British treated India as a single country and made others regard it that way too.

3. Religion, language, art, outlook, a sense of belonging to one people, and historical kinship—these are the characteristics of a people. Viewed in this way, India contains many distinct peoples. They may be grouped into three broad divisions: Dravidians, Muslims and Aryans. Of these three peoples, the Dravidians and Muslims do not differ greatly in their collective characteristics. Between the characteristics of the Aryans and those of the other two there is no compatibility whatever; there is considerable antagonism. Because these distinct collective characteristics exist, only a division of India according to its peoples will give each its own territory and government. Otherwise, the other peoples will have to live in subjection to whichever people is most skilled in cunning, deceit and intrigue, and most disposed to crush others for its own advantage.

4. Continuing to call India a single country allows Aryan domination to grow. Aryan rule has reduced the interests of the other peoples to dust.

5. Binding together, by intrigue, peoples with conflicting characteristics has only increased unrest, distress and trouble. Therefore, if future troubles are not to multiply and turn India into a wilderness of blood, India must now be divided amicably according to its peoples.

6. Dividing a country according to its peoples is neither new nor unheard of. Already there are many Indias: British India, princely India, French India and Dutch India. It is not wrong to ask, in the same way, for three separate regions—Muslim India, Aryan India and Dravidian India.

7. There are 574 princely states alone. Each has its own government and its own system. Similarly, it is an undeniable right for three great regions, each with its own form of government, to seek the means of developing their respective collective characteristics.

### Image 4: 004-219e0fede6.png

8. Just as Turkey, freed from the bonds of the Ottoman Empire, became foremost among the great powers, each region will shine with its own distinction if India is divided according to its peoples.

9. Separate regions would make it possible for each to develop military strength suited to its own characteristics.

10. Even in the time of emperors such as Ashoka, Kanishka, Harsha, Samudragupta and Akbar, India was not a single country. Even then, there was a separate country called Dravida Nadu.

11. If separate regions are formed, each can develop its economy according to the resources available there, and the cruelty of one region exploiting others can be ended.

12. Only when each people has its own territory and government can it enjoy equal rights and equal standing with the others.

13. It was by declaring India a single country that the Aryans made the land from the Himalayas to Cape Kumari their hunting ground. Thus arose a situation in which they could be officials in government, teachers in education, preceptors in religion, gods on earth in society, and people who enjoyed economic ease without labour, while the other peoples languished as servants and workers, worn beyond recognition by their toil. The best way to remove this cruelty is to set apart a region for each group!

14. One people has no trust in another. The mere suggestion that one people should live under the government of another has come to arouse fear. Terrible revolution is the child born of fear and distrust. We say that separation must take place now precisely to prevent this terrible revolution.

15. There is no evidence that India's remaining undivided has so far increased its military, economic or intellectual strength, or created a new society called the Indian people. The reason one people has not

### Image 5: 005-90cc3c5c45.png

crushed another's throat and strangled it to death is simply that British guns held all the peoples down at the same time. Therefore, once the British grip is gone, India will become a battlefield. The wars that frequently occur in Europe provide an example. Thus, to prevent such suffering, the way to avert danger is to divide the territory according to its peoples.

If India is divided according to its peoples, there is consequently a way for each people's characteristics to stand independently and with their own distinction; for Aryan domination to be curbed; for economic exploitation to end; for the distress of intellectual repression to disappear; for future quarrels to be avoided; and for tranquillity and peace to prevail. That is why those whose good intention is that India should not become a battlefield of peoples say that India must be divided according to its peoples. Those who deny this must have forgotten history, or must think that the public does not know it. Only those with the evil intention that one people's character—that is, Aryan domination—should trample, batter and destroy the others, and those with the ruinous notion of making India like a woman married off to the strongest man while somehow securing a livelihood for themselves, will oppose this scheme of separation.

We set out these and similar reasons, with evidence showing how Aryan domination grew, the cruelty by which it destroyed other characteristics, and the necessity of curbing Aryanism from now on. We explained why the arguments of those opposing this programme were untenable. We said that we must perform our duty without being deterred by our opponents' attempts to overthrow reasons with stories, suppress evidence with mere prattle, knock down history with bluster, and destroy determination by growling threats.

The Muslims understood these truths, worked according to their leader's programme, and achieved victory. In the North, Pakistan, a state for their people, has come into being.

### Image 6: 006-2c9488667e.png

The ideal for which they fought and won was ours before they made it their slogan. Before they adopted the Pakistan resolution at Lahore, we had resolved: Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone. Throughout the country we campaigned to explain this ideal... and we continue to do so.

There are more reasons, and stronger ones, for demanding a separate Dravidian government than those advanced for the necessity of Pakistan. Yet we see no sign that the great Dravidian people have understood this even now.

**The Battle Sword**

At Tiruchirappalli! We assembled there the following week. There were no figures of royal pomp there! No great landed proprietors, no zamindars in gold-laced caps! In the conference pavilion, cloth cradles had been tied up for the mothers' little treasures to sleep in. In Congress, they put up with the crooked antics of a king seated in state because he had given two thousand; here, we saw mothers kiss their crying children and keep them from making a sound, and we felt pride and joy. There, those who call themselves champions of patriotism raised a mighty noise in pursuit of votes; here, we undertook the good work of finding a healing herb for a people lying stricken. There, they assembled to find a way for kings to rule; here, they found a way for knowledge to grow. There, seekers of office gathered; here, champions gathered who pledged to bear any hardship or loss to wipe away their people's degradation. There, it was a festival of people dressed in white; here, it was an assembly of young people who vowed: “Henceforth we shall wear black to signify the degradation of this sorrow-stricken land, and begin the struggle for liberation.” There, the harvest; here, the ploughing.

### Image 7: 007-9bf80ef852.png

There, bargaining; here, bravery. There, Congress; here, the Dravidar Kazhagam. In wealthy Bombay, there was the Taj Hotel, the Royal Restaurant, and so many delightful lodgings! So many conveniences! All the pleasures the rich require! Here, a shed in the open! Two miles between the shed and the town! The conference organizers provided no meals. There was not enough lodging. The trouble caused by the rain would not end. Thunder, lightning and the officialdom's heavy hand were all abundant! There were no maharajas or crowned queens at the conference. No fashionable men and no wax dolls to rule over them! Just one motorcar ran here and there—and even that was hired!

Did they lay out a path to office? No! They said: “Give up the office you hold, or stand aside!” Were there preparations for elections? No! They were not to be touched—by Tiruneelakantam! Were there former ministers? None were to be seen! Great landed proprietors? No! Were there people bearing the burdens of the ‘most gracious’ government? No! Who else was there? A flood of Tamils! What course did they devise together? They declared: “We have learned that the country we inhabit is ours; we have also learned that the right to it is ours alone.” They sang: “Take up the killing sword, that the deeds of the most cruel may cease!” They asked: “Aryan rule in the sacred Dravidian land? And a Brahmin drum to proclaim it?” They condemned the government's readiness to accommodate religious orthodoxy. They chose the great elder as the leader fit for this mighty force and cried out for him to show them the field of struggle. They pledged themselves ready for every severe restriction; they assumed the bearing of combatants and departed. In their own villages and hamlets they will say: “We have inscribed our names in the Dravidians' army of liberation! We do not know when the call will come. When it comes, we shall not remain idle. It may be a flowering grove where the cuckoo calls, or a prison that seeks to trap us. We do not know what it will be! But we have pledged ourselves ready for anything.” Tiruchirappalli has displayed its ranks of champions! Inadequate railway facilities,

### Image 8: 008-cf86cca3ab.png

bad weather—despite all these difficulties, the roughly forty thousand people who assembled there constitute the army for our country's liberation. No obstacle can stop it.

After Sir Shanmuganar, one of the eminent scholars and a commentator on the *Silappathikaram*, thundered that the party had lost its purpose; after abuse had been hurled at the sun until tongues were scarred; after the sunai-vizhungis, wriggling in their holes, shot their verbal arrows week after week, saying, “We have toppled this old man”; after the champions in fighting trunks and the idlers seeking refuge had spat fire; after the Sirs and Diwan Bahadurs had feasted, proclaimed their honours, drunk their fill of heroic sentiment and passionately declared, “We shall not give in”; after the lovers of discord had spoken of a split in the party; after those with weighty voices had said, “Only the wild shouters are left; where are the respectable people?”; after they had mocked the party as having dissolved—after all this came this sight! Many thousands of people assembled, knowing that there was none of the attraction or savour of titles, offices or elections; knowing that prison and other trials awaited them; knowing that Periyar was accused of acting dictatorially and refusing to heed anyone; knowing that some had listed the party's defects as a lack of organization, a lack of English and Tamil daily newspapers, a failure to go into Andhra and Karnataka, and a lack of paid campaigners. We say that this gathering is a sight that will open the eyes and minds of a few people who are going astray! After Salem, some printed in bold letters that the party had split, Periyar's strength had dwindled and the split had grown, and imagined that his influence had thereby withered away. The sight of this Tiruchirappalli conference gives their minds a difficult problem! Why did so many people gather despite so many inconveniences? Is this what a split in the party produces—if the split is real? Some of the familiar figures were absent: gentlemen who consulted omens before coming, sent their retainers ahead, required a bungalow to stay in and a company of ‘servants’ fit for a royal audience,

### Image 9: 009-8ed422c5ed.png

who wore turbans on their heads and gold fittings on their walking sticks. Those gentlemen were not there, were they? But there were lions ready to roar and fight!! Our friend Vedachalam expressed it thus:

“Twelve years ago, a Justice Conference was held here in Tiruchirappalli, with the Raja of Ramanathapuram as president and Pethachi Chettiar, the ornament of the wealthy merchant community, as chairman of the reception committee. Today I am the reception chairman—an ordinary man! Periyar is the president. He is no king!”

His words echoed ten million hearts! Yes! When today's Dravidar Kazhagam was called the Justice Party, kings and zamindars treated the party as their own throne room. Today it has become an assembly of the people! Tomorrow, confronted by the great champions of that people's assembly, the party men who today rush about singing Atana will sing and weep: “Yellow has turned black! Our castles in the air have crumbled!” This was once a party inhabited by ‘Honourables’ who sorrowfully wrung their hands and said, “We cannot bear the resolutions adopted at the conference.” At Tiruchirappalli, however, there were thousands upon thousands of champions who heaved a sigh and shrugged their shoulders, saying: “The resolutions are not radical enough. We caught the scent of battle and heard the drum. But where is the order to take the field? Why is it missing?”

A three-storey house: the headquarters of those said to be our party's founding fathers! The anti-Hindi struggle was at its height. At a meeting held then, those engaged in the struggle said that the Justice Party should take it up and conduct it. No sooner had they spoken than the coolness vanished and fury ran riot. “Is this what we have assembled for?” came the question. “We cannot do it,” they said. Who said this? The gentlemen who sent young people to prison to be ‘ruined,’ while they themselves used their wealth to enter the legislature and lead elegant lives! ‘Salem’ should have happened that very day! Periyar suffered, unable either to chew or to swallow. It was already clear that these were materials that would not stick together. With the adhesive called consideration for others,

### Image 10: 010-b5f69c39ac.png

he made them stick together! But that served merely to hold them together, not to blast the Aryan autocracy that destroys Dravidam's honour. What happened at the Salem Conference—‘Salem’—should have happened long before. It happened somewhat late! Even afterwards, some people, including some of our own, were doubtful and troubled: had it really brought us a loss? Tiruchirappalli has wiped away that doubt! Our path is now clear; all that remains is to begin the journey. Soon! It will certainly begin!!

This is the concrete benefit we gained from the Tiruchirappalli conference! We have lost nothing by the departure of our former associates. “The electoral mushroom put together from those old associates cannot obstruct our path! Our path has been cleared”—that is the lesson Tiruchirappalli has taught us. There is no longer room for hesitation, for looking back, for a faltering voice or for the trouble caused by excessive consideration for others! Nor is there any need for them. We stand upon the path of struggle and look about us! As far as the eye can see, there are comrades with fearless hearts, comrades carried beyond themselves by enthusiasm, comrades of great ability! They watch *The Battle Sword* and delight in it! Until midnight, forgetting home for the country's sake, men and women, children and old people sat together in the open, without a thought of whether rain might pour down. Why? To prove false the sneer of certain gentlemen and their ‘liveried servants’: “We have left; now these people have no one!” With this multitude on our side, we cannot refrain from beginning the struggle for our rights. That struggle will certainly begin! The time is not far away!!

When Congress emerged as a very powerful majority, the Raja of Panagal said: “Until now, you have seen only the spectacle of a majority party governing. Watch: I shall show you a strange spectacle! A party in the minority,

## Part 3

### Image 1: 001-5b46a9b58f.png

making the majority party dance to its tune and governing—you will now see that!” And he demonstrated it. The Congressmen, who had threatened the Europeans, declaring that they had obtained the right to govern, became puppets dancing at Panagal's word. Today Periyar says: “Until now, you have seen only the exploits of a party that contests and wins an election, and those of the party that sits in the legislature opposing it. Now you are going to witness a wonder. What is it? The Dravidian party will not contest elections. Yet, from outside the legislature, it will be a fiercer and more effective opposition than it could be from within, enough to subdue the swagger of the party that wins an election and comes joyfully to govern. Watch me accomplish this wonder!” No one who saw the Tiruchirappalli conference could think this impossible! With so many brave people, how can courage be lacking? What does it matter if Periyar's great army does not enter the legislature? We shall turn the whole country into a legislature! Those who come to govern will be brought before the people's assembly! That is certain! “Am I the one governing, or is this Ramasamy Naicker?” Achariar asked arrogantly. “Are we to withdraw Hindi for fear of one Ramasamy?” he said angrily. But, poor man, that same Achariar cried out: “They are emerging like winged termites from an anthill! Had I known there would be so much trouble over this accursed thing, I would never have introduced Hindi!” What had he seen? Did our party have a noteworthy number of members in the legislature? No! What else had brought sorrow to that Brahmin? The opposition that had flared up throughout the country! If this was possible when many thousands had not yet been formally enrolled as party members, when branches had not been formed, and when our voice was unheard in the colleges, then today, when members are flocking in, branches are flourishing here and there, and commanders are being trained in the colleges; today, when the news that Thalamuthu and Natarasan had become corpses has heated our blood; in these days when the revolutionary poet's new ideas heal our wounds and instil a new courage,

### Image 2: 002-cdf79145da.png

in these days when outstanding actors put their art to work for Dravida Nadu's liberation; when connoisseurs of art labour to patch the holes and cracks in order to save Kamban; when *The Hindu* has been obliged to report that Cochin, Travancore, Mysore and Andhra sent representatives to the provincial conference; when even the homes of gentlemen who never forget to wear their sacred forehead marks have acquired a firm resolve against ‘Brahmin pollution’; when champions have assembled who angrily complain that there are no resolutions throwing off sparks of fire; when men like Kamaraj can say that Gandhi's relative by marriage slipped dishonestly into Congress—in these days, we ask why we cannot, without entering the legislatures ourselves, make those who enter them govern the country as we wish. So much was possible then! And now!! The very thought brings joy mingled with confidence, like a mixture with fruit juice!

Let whoever wishes come to govern. We tell the people of the country, pledging our own strength, that we shall make them behave like ‘good children.’ We can certainly do it!!

Wavell brought his forces back from the Burmese battlefield to India. Those who knew nothing of military strategy mocked him! Then? The Andaman Islands were given up. Those ignorant of the art of war sneered. Today? If commanders scatter their forces, or attack without understanding that there is a time for an offensive, their army will not survive and victory will not be won. Let the Congress Tojo seize Malaya, Java, the Philippines and Pearl Harbor—why be afraid? We shall strike Tokyo when the time is right! After that blow, the halls of victorious pomp turn into places where instruments of surrender must be signed. Why worry? But some will say that the Allies could later achieve victory, in the Western governments and in the Eastern governments alike, only because the Russian army fought the Nazi army fiercely, regardless of hardship and loss. True! Just

### Image 3: 003-7cac60f855.png

so, in today's electoral struggle, the Muslim League is the Russian army!! What doubt can there be? The Muslim League is going to prove that Congress has no exclusive proprietary right over India! Thus, our not participating in this election is not harmful, nor will it make Congress all-powerful. Our weapons of struggle are in disrepair! Let us put them right; then we shall spring forward! Just as a powerful blow to the head brought about the surrender of all the places Japan had seized, without the need to attack and recapture every one of them, we who stand aside today can subdue the advancing Congress! Let us give that party an opportunity to govern, so that its arrogance may perish! Some fail to understand this subtlety of strategy because of the worn-out enthusiasm that ‘election brokers’ seek out and supply to them—nothing else! At the Tiruchirappalli conference, we made this clear too.

We reached another important decision: we have formed an army of liberation to fight to wipe away social degradation. Periyar said that its members should wear a ‘black shirt.’ He also explained why. The clothing, he said, was a sign of mourning for the degraded condition into which the Dravidian people had fallen. We have lost our country and our government; the ordinary people among us have lost understanding; we have lost the richness of our language, the distinction of our art, our wealth and our dignity, and lie oppressed as the fourth caste. The cruelty inflicted upon us by a small group causes anguish. Still greater sorrow comes from the fact that many of our own people do not recognize that cruelty. It is as a sign of this great sorrow that our leader asks us to wear black. Our enemies will add fresh abuse to their existing ridicule: ‘black-shirted fellow,’ ‘one dressed like a thief.’ Let their desire to do so be satisfied. Those who find socialism only on paper will say that the black shirt is a symbol of fascism. Let them speak. Let our clothing make our sorrow known to the country! This army of liberation will, in the struggle against social

### Image 4: 004-60d9e6bdfe.png

degradation, stand in the forefront! Those who use their crooked minds to prolong this cruel condition would do well to understand the enthusiasm Tiruchirappalli has given it. The awakening witnessed at the conference over what social degradation is, and how and by what means it should be abolished, shows the new stage the party has reached.

In earlier days, even though men such as Dr Nair proclaimed that this party existed precisely for social reform, the introduction of a progressive, somewhat radical resolution at a conference would cause trembling. For example, if someone spoke of admitting Adi Dravidian people into temples, there were those among us who cried out “Hara Hara! Shiva Shiva!”, those who lamented “Rama! Rama!”, those who reasoned that we would get no votes if we went to the electorate after saying this, and those who warned against being so revolutionary. At Tiruchirappalli, a complete reversal! A resolution demanded that Adi Dravidians be permitted to enter every temple. There was no orthodox opposition, no commotion from defenders of the old religious order, no fear from lovers of antiquity! What was there instead? Many comrades spoke thus: “Temple entry will do Adi Dravidians no good! It is enough that we have ruined ourselves by going there. Do not send the original inhabitants there and ruin them too! What is there in the temples? They are Aryan fortresses! Dens of thieves! Settlements of religious orthodoxy! Traps of ritualism! We are engaged in rescuing people from precisely that orthodox grip. Why, then, should we make Adi Dravidians go into temples and ruin their lives and understanding?” They spoke not merely from a mixture of enthusiasm and passion, but from a real conviction that temple entry would do harm. The age when people said, “Allowing Adi Dravidians into temples will harm religion, endanger religious observance—it is a terrible sin!” has passed away. An age has dawned in which people ask, “Why, sir, are you sending Adi Dravidians into that den of thieves? Why impose the punishment of temple entry on those innocent people?” A temple! Here,

### Image 5: 005-a952e0a957.png

formerly, the sign said: “Adi Dravidians must not enter.” Now our comrades are preparing a sign that says: “This is a temple! Adi Dravidian comrades, do not go inside! The Aryan lurks within to rob you of your understanding and your wealth.” In the end, the resolution was adopted only after it was explained that demanding temple entry was part of the struggle for rights, not a means of thrusting them into the Aryan religion! Such is the surging enthusiasm for Self-Respect, such the radical outlook! How can Rao Bahadurs remain amid this ferment? They would be scorched! “All the temples will become shelters for the poor! Reading rooms! Beautiful places with fresh air and light!” said Comrade Neelamegam, who opened the Self-Respect Conference. The applause with which the delegates greeted those words could have shaken the Aryan fortresses! Such enthusiasm! Such an awakening! Each of the leaders' spirited speeches deserves separate explanation. Yet one fundamental principle ran through them all. T. Shanmugam, president of the Self-Respect Conference, and Comrade Arjunan, who opened the Dravidian Conference, stated it clearly: Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone! In his excellent speech, Comrade Arjunan explained the dangerous economic domination the North had acquired. In his great address, Comrade Shanmugam eloquently showed that Periyar was the man capable of gathering and leading a great force to break that domination. The Tiruchirappalli conferences showed the form of a Dravidam awakened and aroused! They were pictures that enhanced Periyar's influence, scenes demonstrating that the world of youth would never forget a leader who worked with determination or hesitate to follow him. Unforgettable scenes! Scenes that could show discerning people what Dravidam's future would be! Tiruchirappalli has discharged its duty in a manner deserving the highest praise. Our friend Vedachalam, accustomed to asking in a somewhat fearful voice, “Can we do it?”,

### Image 6: 006-0e6ee21d53.png

achieved the success of those conferences through his perseverance! Beside him stood Periyar's volunteer corps from Ponmalai, Tiruchirappalli, Thanjavur and many other places. Every region eagerly came forward to contribute to the conference's success, producing a remarkable triumph. When the Tiruchirappalli conference assembled, Periyar was sixty-seven. When it ended, he was forty-five. Such ‘youthful vigour’ had come upon him from hearing the young people's rousing voices!! During the preparations, many difficulties and sudden dangers arose along the way. They all became like the joints in a stalk of sugarcane! We warmly commend the comrades of Tiruchirappalli. Having earned the distinction of successfully conducting so great a conference, we trust that Tiruchirappalli will become a centre of endeavour, a training ground and a camp for making Dravida Nadu belong to the Dravidians, and will show Dravidam the way. Thanking everyone who helped in various ways to make the conference a success, we conclude with: Long live Dravidam! The Tiruchirappalli conference has truly given the Dravidians a battle sword! Dravidian champions assembled to wield the sword fearlessly. They have kissed the battle sword their leader gave them and grasped it in their hands! In former days, Justice conferences gave young people pen nibs with which to apply for government jobs. At Tiruchirappalli, what was placed in the young people's hands was a battle sword!! To cut down the Aryan cactus spreading through Dravidam; to hack to pieces the deceit that twists and writhes under the name of religious orthodoxy; to sever and cast away the poisonous vine of ignorance massed within people's minds; to destroy the thorny thicket of caste distinctions; to kill the cruelty called antiquated custom that embraces champions and makes them useless; to rescue Dravidam—at Tiruchirappalli, a battle sword was placed in the hands of brave Dravidians.

### Image 7: 007-981f29770d.png

**The History of the Ideal's Growth**

**The Ideal Is Our Fundamental Slogan**

The ideal “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone” has now become our fundamental slogan. All the many other questions receive attention only insofar as they help support that ideal. We hold this ideal as our single objective. Though members of the Dravidar Kazhagam have many different views and programmes concerning religion, society and economics, all these are arranged to accord with the ideal “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone,” like the underlying pitch in music.

**A Day to Affirm Our Resolve**

Dravida Nadu Separation Day is being organized to declare our determination never to abandon this ideal, and to state firmly that, until it is fulfilled, we cannot be satisfied with the small change thrown our way. On this occasion, we wish to bring to mind how our ideal, “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone,” developed.

**The Champion Who Gave His Life Proclaiming Our Rights**

Thirty years ago, the assertion of our rights as Dravidians arose here. But that cry was corrupted by its association with the inhabitants of mansions. Until his last breath, Dr C. Natesa Mudaliar proclaimed before the people: “This country is Dravida Nadu—we who live here are Dravidians.” Instead of using the momentum created by the remarkable movement he began for genuine liberation, the gentlemen who conducted politics seated in palaces used that momentum to obtain titles and offices. They also intended that all their abilities should be used to find fault with Congress and, at the same time, to sing the praises of the British,

### Image 8: 008-ca3493632a.png

and that was their intention. Thus, the assertion of rights in the word ‘Dravidian,’ instead of becoming a battle song for establishing a Dravidian government, became a self-interested noise knocking at the doors of government offices. Dr Natesa Mudaliar died broken-hearted; throughout his life he never failed to proclaim himself a Dravidian.

**Leaders Who Showed No Enthusiasm for Removing Aryanism**

In those days, the English names S. I. L. F. and Justice circulated in the mansions, but before the people the name used was Dravidian. The Tamil newspaper itself was named *Dravidan*. Dravidian art, Dravidian religion, Dravidian medicine, Dravidian musicians—in those days, many people in many fields sought to work for a Dravidian renaissance. But the leaders failed to take an interest in that foundational work. They spent all their energies chasing the illusory deer called political power. Thus, when they met prominent people in each town, they spoke about elections and emphasized that the Iyers dominated government employment and that this domination must be ended. They did not work to remove the Aryanism embedded in society or to instil Dravidian consciousness.

**The Leaders of ‘Those Days’ Became a Puzzle to the People**

The leaders of those days said openly that Brahmin domination in society was no danger; it was only the Brahmins who possessed political power who posed a danger to them. Apart from one or two such as Dr Natesa Mudaliar, that was how the leaders spoke. Meanwhile, men such as J. N. Ramanathan, Kannappar and T. V. Subramanian, who worked among the people and campaigned, instilled a sense of being Dravidian and explained and condemned Brahmin domination not only in politics but also in society. The leaders, however, used the people only to the extent of securing their votes against the Brahmins. Consequently,

### Image 9: 009-2e72870961.png

at first the people did not understand those leaders' conduct. Once they understood it, they disliked it; disliking it, they withdrew.

**The Mansion-Dwellers Hunt the Illusory Deer**

The first effort to awaken Dravidian collective consciousness was defeated not because the people failed to become interested, but because our leaders attended only to politics and thought it wrong to intervene in society. Some twenty years were spent hunting the illusory deer—arranging ministerial offices, in other words pursuing self-interest and, as a consequence, plotting conspiracies. In that period, the mansions became halls of conspiracy! Promoters of personalities became political puppet-masters! Office-seekers mounted the stage! The European too had cause to celebrate. The people, clearly realizing that this business was not being conducted for them, began to condemn the party itself. Intrigue in the mansions grew beyond control; one leader made it his occupation to bring down another, and, striking at one another, they fell together in a heap. In that shock, the Dravidian consciousness that had first arisen with enthusiasm grew dim; it did not die. That consciousness has never died—it has often dimmed—for it is the voice of the heart, not the din of political grasshoppers.

**Dravidam Suffering in Aryanism's Embrace**

For a long time, Dravidam has repeatedly tried to free itself from Aryanism's embrace. Though it has sometimes been inactive, it has never surrendered completely. The movements that have arisen from time to time under such headings as Pure Tamil, Vellala civilization, true Saivism and ancient civilization are all small versions of that fundamental endeavour. Each is necessary and each has its benefits. Yet, because only a few among the people could understand them, these movements could not strengthen the fundamental endeavour. Each became a separate movement. They even came to appear contradictory. Feelings of hostility too

### Image 10: 010-8e7b643f78.png

grew. Many camps began to appear in Tamil Nadu! The fundamental endeavour was forgotten.

**Opponents Rejoiced at the Apparent Death of That Consciousness**

The country was like a blindfolded warrior walking along a road, stumbling painfully into tree after tree. It became doubtful whether Dravidian collective consciousness would ever arise again. Opponents even rejoiced that it had died altogether. But the ideal had not died—it was waiting for its moment. Many occasions arose, but none was the right one.

**A Downtrodden Tamil Nadu Awoke to Enthusiasm for Itself**

The anti-Hindi struggle provided the right moment. It was a time when not merely anger against Congress—that was relatively slight—but love of Tamil spread throughout the country. It was a time when people everywhere began to read Sangam works. They began to speak proudly of the Tamil's own language and distinctive character; discussion of the Five Great Epics reached the public platform! As enthusiasm for Tamil increased, the expounders of Puranic lore slipped in too. But those passionate about Tamil began to investigate why the language had been brought low, why Tamil character had disappeared, and why Tamil Nadu's distinction had declined, preventing the others' influence from growing. That investigation brought them back to the point where they had left off—to the vicinity of Dr Natesa Mudaliar's grave. Old memories and old battle songs began to circulate again!

**The Champions Who ‘Dug Channels’ to Revive the Withering Tamil Crop**

The sight of more than a thousand people going to prison to protect the Tamil language awakened a new feeling in Tamil hearts. Tamils shed tears at the graves of Thalamuthu and Natarajan. Two young men dead, many young men imprisoned, all for the protection of Tamil: it was when they stood beside those graves and reflected on this that the resolve to change this condition

## Part 4

### Image 1: 001-8491d3249c.png

was born in the Tamils. It was then that Periyar Ramasamy proclaimed: “Comrade, you cry out that another language threatens Tamil! Think about why that danger arose and who brought it about. Is the danger only to your language? Your art, your way of life, your culture, your people, your country and your very livelihood are threatened. Have you understood that? Do you know who brought this danger upon you? Will you consider why it arose? Listen, I shall tell you. Tamil Nadu is not in Tamil hands! That is why Tamil wealth is not in Tamil hands. Whatever a man from another country thinks now becomes law here. This land becomes a market for his goods. This condition must change. A language struggle alone is not enough. This is not the end; it is the beginning. Driving out Hindi alone is not enough: all the evil forces that dominate Tamil Nadu must disappear. That must be our slogan. Henceforth that is our struggle, our ideal—Tamil Nadu for the Tamils alone!” “Yes! Yes! We have understood! We see clearly!” cried the great gathering on the seashore that day, its numbers approaching a hundred thousand. They pledged themselves to the cause. The waves sounded as though expressing the turmoil in the people's hearts. Above, the moon seemed to illuminate the Tamils' awakened state! Clouds here and there in the sky were like blemishes in Tamil society! The Tamil had regained his ideal. He proclaimed: Tamil Nadu for the Tamils alone. The ideal that had grown dim began to move among the people again! It reached them! Thus the ideal acquired a new strength. People became eager to work for an ideal. They began to think: we are going to fight not for mansions or offices, but for our country's independent rights! The revolutionary poet's golden words nourished that thought still further:

“Let us dance, let us sing the pallu song!
Let us gather in the glory of Tamil rule!”

So the poet said. Joy began to dance in the eyes of thousands upon thousands of young people. Elections, legislatures, ministries

### Image 2: 002-6a35a0d7e5.png

and the like appeared utterly ordinary in their eyes. Those who hankered after such things began to look like caricatures to the young people at Salem. Overcoming obstacles and difficulties, the young began to walk towards that ideal. Delighted to have found a new path, the Tamil realized that he must recover his country to protect his language and art. The battle cry he raised was: Tamil Nadu for the Tamils alone!

**Those Who Sneered Encountered a ‘Volcano’**

“Rat-holes for the rats alone,” said the clever men who thought they could destroy anything merely by ridicule! The people, however, began to repeat: Tamil Nadu for the Tamils alone. “It is true, is it not? Just, is it not? Necessary, is it not?” they said to one another, and went on thinking: what is Tamil Nadu like today? They saw everything permeated by Aryanism. They began to investigate how long this condition had existed, and learned that it dated from the day the Tamil made room for Aryanism. They examined the condition before that, and discovered that beautiful Dravidam had flourished in strength, protected by seas on three sides and the Vindhyas to the north. They saw that one part of Dravidam had become Andhra and another Kerala; that, though separated, these regions possessed a linguistic unity. They recalled ancient Dravidam and found that the word ‘Dravidam’ stood as the opposite of Aryanism, a sign of a culture entirely different from that called Aryan. They found history, geography and the study of people's mental characteristics supporting this truth! “Oh! We have found the truth!” they said. “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone!” The ideal took its full form! Today, some who support that ideal but feel embarrassed to say “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians alone” speak of provincial freedom or provincial autonomy. Others say: “Full rights for us—but must we not have an all-India connection?” It is not the twists and turns in their words that chiefly deserve

### Image 3: 003-273523f939.png

our attention; what matters is that they are moving towards the ideal.

**His Delusion Cleared, Yet He Refused to Think!**

The president of the Tamil Nadu Congress chose the time of our Dravidian Separation Day celebrations to say: “The programme that Dravida Nadu should become a separate country will only divide the country further.” In other words, our friend Kamaraj tells us what the consequence of our programme will be. He refuses to consider why it is needed—or perhaps he has thought about it, discovered the truth and is concealing it! How angry Kamaraj became with the high command when northern leaders interfered in our affairs! He should consider whether it is a crime for us to be angry when that same high command intervenes and establishes its dominance in internal affairs, art, civilization and, above all, economics. Amid his many difficulties it may be hard to find time; nevertheless, if he makes a little time to speak with our leader, he can learn why we demand that Dravida Nadu become a separate country. It would be appropriate for him to state his opinion afterwards. It is not the right method to consider a question by first imagining its consequences for oneself.

The day on which we invite Kamaraj and others like him to consider this question and examine the reasons is 1 July.

**Why Does ‘Self-Interest’ Appear in Separate Sovereignty?**

The man who said that Dravidastan would split the country nevertheless approaches our ideal at the same time, knowingly or unknowingly! He has said: “Andhra, Kerala and Tamil Nadu must live together with the Indian federation as separate independent entities.” He says INDEPENDENT UNITS—units possessing rights of their own. Why did this desire arise? What is the reason for seeking separate rights? What is the difference between the present condition and the condition that would follow from obtaining those rights?


### Image 4: 004-81272e1cc9.png

He did not explain this! Moreover, he conceals the fact that, contrary to his desire, these “units” are now being impaired in the Constituent Assembly—because he cannot prevent it! He asks for separate sovereignty; we ask that it be free of blemish or defect. He says, “Even after obtaining separate sovereignty, we must join the Indian federation.” Not only the Indian federation—even if it is the Asian federation of which Nehru speaks, or the world federation of which Wendell Willkie spoke, we shall join; there is nothing wrong with that, and no obstacle to it. But we must have separate sovereignty! Where is the plan for that? They are drawing up a plan in the Constituent Assembly! Yet it cannot fulfil Kamaraj’s aspiration! What does our friend say to that?

**We Have Seen Even Opponents Speak of the “Ideal”**

Before saying that there must be no Dravidastan, Congress leaders in responsible positions should ask why it is being demanded and learn the answer. The day of celebration is an invitation to do so. We are glad to find a reflection of our ideal even in the words of friends who say they oppose it. What accounts for this situation? The ideal has grown—we find its fragrance in all sorts of unexpected places. If, for some time more, we explain our ideal, particularly the economic reasons for it, “Dravida Nadu for the Dravidians” will cease to be merely a party slogan and become the country’s slogan. The ideal that has grown despite so many obstacles will indeed emerge in this full form. For this ideal is the drumbeat of an awakened people’s rights, not party talk.

**We Have Endured! Later We Shall Gird Ourselves for the Contest**

Whenever pictures of Ramakrishna, printed in Calcutta, reach us and we hang them on a wall plastered with Dalmia cement, driving in a nail sent by the Tata company, this ideal will quietly stand beside our ear and ask, “Is this your condition? Is there no iron in your country?” Having asked, it will laugh! When dhotis and saris from Sholapur and

### Image 5: 005-25e5cad7e8.png

Bombay arrive here in greater quantities because they cannot go to Lahore and Vishawar, the ideal will come to mind! Medicines arriving from the Bengal chemical works will call attention to the ideal! When trade and contact with the outside world stop with those north of the Vindhyas, the ideal will come to mind. At first there will be a little hesitation; then courage will arise. From all sorts of places we did not expect, people will voice the ideal: “We cannot endure this northern domination. Dravida Nadu must belong to the Dravidians.” It is to see that day that we endure the humiliations, accusations, opposition and mockery of these days—and shall continue to endure them. It is not we who matter here, but the country!

**There Must Be No Attitude of Worship**

“We are engaged in the good work of redeeming the country. There are now only two parties here: one is the party waging the war of liberation, and the other is imperialism. Therefore there must be no other parties. After the country gains freedom, when it is time to govern, there may be parties; there must be none during the struggle.” So Congress leaders had been saying. Today the country has obtained freedom. Yet Congressmen have developed a strong conviction that there must be no other party! They alone must govern the country, enjoying a monopoly without opposition; however harmful the administration may be, no one must oppose it.

The elation of victory is the chief reason for this perverse idea, but its root cause lies elsewhere.

They believe they can govern only if everyone tolerates whatever mistakes they make and there is no one even to expose those mistakes; if many people, or even a few, have the opportunity to find fault,


### Image 6: 006-5e5d1efbc9.png

they fear that their “grip” will weaken. That fear is the root cause.

This can damage the principle of republican government.

The scholar Dr Krishnalal Seetharani has explained, with sound reasons, why this tendency must be avoided.

A new situation has arisen in the country; he says that democracy will not grow if the old “attitude of worship” continues now. His comments are published below.

**New Parties Are Needed**

“At this time when we have attained freedom, we need more than one political party. Wherever a single party exercises dominance, democracy’s dwelling place is the grave. This will be a great test, for in the course of the long, arduous struggle we have waged, we have learned to distrust every party except Congress. But we must recognize that the reason for our devotion to Congress alone is that, until now, Congress has been a movement rather than a party. It functioned as a popular front; under its auspices, different currents of thought united to formulate a common ideal against foreign authority. That movement has now fulfilled its task of freeing India from foreign domination.

“At this moment of attaining freedom, Congress becomes a party. Its character as a movement disappears. This situation makes it necessary to establish other parties with differing political and economic programmes.

“Some of these new parties will arise from within Congress itself. Those parties will also admit people who have never given Congress their unswerving loyalty. Congress appears likely to split into a right wing and a left wing.

### Image 7: 007-4af8927757.png

“Nevertheless, it too will continue to exercise dominance for some time as a party of the centre.

**Freedom of the Press**

“At this time when we have gained freedom, we also need a free press. There is a difficult dilemma here. Some people will think that our newspapers will automatically become free at the very moment foreign repression is abolished. But they may behave independently under foreign rule; it is under our own rule that behaving in this way is difficult. By praising and supporting our heroes, who entered the struggle against the imperialists with various principles, our newspapers performed the service they owed the nation. This became a habit. Habits are hard to change. If our newspapers voluntarily begin to serve as publicity organizations for our heroes, they will fail in their respective democratic duties. In wartime it is indeed difficult to tolerate criticism of faults and shortcomings. But in peacetime it becomes a duty to point out faults and shortcomings with the intention of bringing about reform.

“Only the press has the power to correct mischief—that is, autocracy. But it has that power only if it is free. Our editors must now behave toward our own leaders with as much courage as they displayed against the foreign ruling class. This may taste as bitter as neem fruit to some of our leaders, for until now their words have gone unchallenged. But it is the right of a democratic people to expect upright conduct from their leaders. No person possessed of the conceit ‘We are everything’ can be qualified to lead a democratic government.


### Image 8: 008-a64d5b73bb.png

“This brings me to my view of hero-worship. During a struggle, whether armed or non-violent, we need a military outlook. Consequently, our hero-worship intensifies; as it advances, our loyalty, previously scattered in many places, becomes concentrated and goes to that one man who stands above everyone else. Wherever we look, his face appears before us. We attribute every virtue to him. No one else can satisfy us.
